In 2018, <a href="/wiki/Coalition_Avenir_Qu%C3%A9bec" title="Coalition Avenir Québec">CAQ</a> <a href="/wiki/National_Assembly_of_Quebec" title="National Assembly of Quebec">MNA</a> and then <a href="/wiki/Journal_de_Montreal" class="mw-redirect" title="Journal de Montreal">Journal de Montreal</a> journalist, <a href="/wiki/Sylvain_L%C3%A9vesque" title="Sylvain Lévesque">Sylvain Lévesque</a>, referred to Mulroney as a political influence when criticizing the relatability of progressive decisions made by Prime Minister Justin Trudeau.<sup id="cite_ref-210" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-210">&#91;205&#93;</a></sup> In that same year, former Bloc Québécois leader <a href="/wiki/Michel_Gauthier" title="Michel Gauthier">Michel Gauthier</a> said he considered Mulroney to be the greatest prime minister of the last 50 years.<sup id="cite_ref-lapresse.ca_211-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-lapresse.ca-211">&#91;206&#93;</a></sup> In 2019, St. Francis Xavier University in Nova Scotia inaugurated the Brian Mulroney Institute of Government, a $100-million initiative designed to provide undergraduates with degrees in public policy and governance.<sup id="cite_ref-G&amp;M_Sept_2019_212-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-G&amp;M_Sept_2019-212">&#91;207&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Supreme_Court_appointments">Supreme Court appointments</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Brian_Mulroney&amp;action=edit&amp;section=35" title="Edit section: Supreme Court appointments"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>Mulroney chose the following jurists to be appointed by the <a href="/wiki/Constitutional_advice" class="mw-redirect" title="Constitutional advice">Governor-in-Council</a> to be <a href="/wiki/Puisne_judge" title="Puisne judge">puisne justices</a> of the <a href="/wiki/Supreme_Court_of_Canada" title="Supreme Court of Canada">Supreme Court of Canada</a>, one of whom, Beverley McLachlin, was subsequently elevated to <a href="/wiki/Chief_Justice_of_Canada" title="Chief Justice of Canada">Chief Justice of Canada</a>:
</p>
<ul><li><a href="/wiki/G%C3%A9rard_La_Forest" title="Gérard La Forest">Gérard La Forest</a> (January 16, 1985 – September 30, 1997)<sup id="cite_ref-213" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-213">&#91;208&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Claire_L%27Heureux-Dub%C3%A9" title="Claire L&#39;Heureux-Dubé">Claire L'Heureux-Dubé</a> (April 15, 1987 – July 1, 2002)<sup id="cite_ref-214" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-214">&#91;209&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/John_Sopinka" title="John Sopinka">John Sopinka</a> (May 24, 1988 – November 24, 1997)<sup id="cite_ref-215" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-215">&#91;210&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Charles_Gonthier" title="Charles Gonthier">Charles Gonthier</a> (February 1, 1989 – August 1, 2003)<sup id="cite_ref-216" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-216">&#91;211&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Peter_Cory" title="Peter Cory">Peter Cory</a> (February 1, 1989 – June 1, 1999)<sup id="cite_ref-217" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-217">&#91;212&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Beverley_McLachlin" title="Beverley McLachlin">Beverley McLachlin</a> (March 30, 1989 – December 15, 2017; subsequently appointed Chief Justice of Canada on the advice of Prime Minister Jean Chrétien, from January 7, 2000)<sup id="cite_ref-218" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-218">&#91;213&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/William_Stevenson_(judge)" title="William Stevenson (judge)">William Stevenson</a> (September 17, 1990 – June 5, 1992)<sup id="cite_ref-219" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-219">&#91;214&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Frank_Iacobucci" title="Frank Iacobucci">Frank Iacobucci</a> (January 7, 1991 – June 30, 2004)<sup id="cite_ref-220" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-220">&#91;215&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/John_C._Major" title="John C. Major">John C. Major</a> (November 13, 1992 – December 25, 2005)<sup id="cite_ref-:2_221-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:2-221">&#91;216&#93;</a></sup></li></ul>
<p>Mulroney also advised the appointment of <a href="/wiki/Antonio_Lamer" title="Antonio Lamer">Antonio Lamer</a> (as Chief Justice, July 1, 1990 – January 6, 2000.  Lamer had been appointed a <a href="/wiki/Puisne_Justice" class="mw-redirect" title="Puisne Justice">Puisne Justice</a> on the advice of Prime Minister Pierre Trudeau, March 28, 1980).<sup id="cite_ref-:2_221-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:2-221">&#91;216&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Honours">Honours</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Brian_Mulroney&amp;action=edit&amp;section=36" title="Edit section: Honours"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>According to Canadian protocol, as a former Prime Minister, he was styled "<a href="/wiki/The_Right_Honourable" title="The Right Honourable">The Right Honourable</a>" for life.
<br />
</p>

<li>Awarded to Mulroney for cementing Canada as the first Western Nation to legitimize <a href="/wiki/Russia%E2%80%93Ukraine_relations" title="Russia–Ukraine relations">Ukrainian independence</a>, only a day after <a href="/wiki/Declaration_of_Independence_of_Ukraine" title="Declaration of Independence of Ukraine">declaration</a>, in 1991.</li></ul>
</td>
<td><sup id="cite_ref-228" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-228">&#91;223&#93;</a></sup>
</td></tr>
<tr>
<td><span typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:JPN_Kyokujitsu-sho_1Class_BAR.svg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c4/JPN_Kyokujitsu-sho_1Class_BAR.svg/80px-JPN_Kyokujitsu-sho_1Class_BAR.svg.png" decoding="async" width="80" height="22" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c4/JPN_Kyokujitsu-sho_1Class_BAR.svg/120px-JPN_Kyokujitsu-sho_1Class_BAR.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c4/JPN_Kyokujitsu-sho_1Class_BAR.svg/160px-JPN_Kyokujitsu-sho_1Class_BAR.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="218" data-file-height="60" /></a></span></td>
<td>Grand Cordon of the <a href="/wiki/Order_of_the_Rising_Sun" title="Order of the Rising Sun">Order of the Rising Sun</a> (Japan)</td>
<td>
<ul><li>2011</li>
<li>Awarded for Mulroney's signing of The Japanese Canadian Redress Agreement, acknowledging the Canadian government's wrongful actions against the Japanese <a href="/wiki/Internment_of_Japanese_Canadians" title="Internment of Japanese Canadians">during World War Two</a>. Mulroney's actions to strengthen <a href="/wiki/Canada%E2%80%93Japan_relations" title="Canada–Japan relations">Japanese-Canadian relations</a> business-wise in the 2000s further complimented the previous reason.</li></ul>
</td>
<td><sup id="cite_ref-229" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-229">&#91;224&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-230" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-230">&#91;225&#93;</a></sup>
</td></tr>
<tr>
<td><span typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:Order_of_the_Companions_of_O.R._Tambo_(ribbon_bar).gif" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4b/Order_of_the_Companions_of_O.R._Tambo_%28ribbon_bar%29.gif/80px-Order_of_the_Companions_of_O.R._Tambo_%28ribbon_bar%29.gif" decoding="async" width="80" height="22" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4b/Order_of_the_Companions_of_O.R._Tambo_%28ribbon_bar%29.gif/120px-Order_of_the_Companions_of_O.R._Tambo_%28ribbon_bar%29.gif 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4b/Order_of_the_Companions_of_O.R._Tambo_%28ribbon_bar%29.gif/160px-Order_of_the_Companions_of_O.R._Tambo_%28ribbon_bar%29.gif 2x" data-file-width="218" data-file-height="60" /></a></span></td>

<td>
<ul><li>2015</li>

</td>

<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Order_of_Canada_Citation">Order of Canada Citation</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Brian_Mulroney&amp;action=edit&amp;section=38" title="Edit section: Order of Canada Citation"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Mulroney was appointed a Companion of the Order of Canada on May 6, 1998. His citation reads:<sup id="cite_ref-245" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-245">&#91;240&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<blockquote><p>As the eighteenth Prime Minister of Canada, he led the country for nine consecutive years. His accomplishments include, among others, the signing of the Free Trade Agreement with the United States, the North American Free Trade Agreement with Mexico and the United States, and the Acid Rain Treaty. In other international activities, he assumed the leadership of the Commonwealth countries against apartheid in South Africa and was appointed Co-chair of the United Nations' World Summit for Children. Fiscal reform, important environmental initiatives and employment equity were also highlights of his political career.</p></blockquote>
<div style="clear:both;" class=""></div>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Other_awards">Other awards</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Brian_Mulroney&amp;action=edit&amp;section=39" title="Edit section: Other awards"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>In 2018, Mulroney was inducted into the <a href="/wiki/Canadian_Disability_Hall_of_Fame" title="Canadian Disability Hall of Fame">Canadian Disability Hall of Fame</a><sup id="cite_ref-246" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-246">&#91;241&#93;</a></sup> and was awarded the <a href="/wiki/George_Bush_Award_for_Excellence_in_Public_Service" class="mw-redirect" title="George Bush Award for Excellence in Public Service">George Bush Award for Excellence in Public Service</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-GB_Award_247-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-GB_Award-247">&#91;242&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Coat_of_arms">Coat of arms</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Brian_Mulroney&amp;action=edit&amp;section=40" title="Edit section: Coat of arms"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<table class="wikitable mw-collapsible" style="max-width: 100%">
<caption class="nowrap">Coat of arms of Brian Mulroney
</caption>
<tbody><tr>
<td style="width: 220px;"><span class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Frameless"><a href="/wiki/File:Brian_Mulroney_Arms.svg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/0e/Brian_Mulroney_Arms.svg/220px-Brian_Mulroney_Arms.svg.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="242" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/0e/Brian_Mulroney_Arms.svg/330px-Brian_Mulroney_Arms.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/0e/Brian_Mulroney_Arms.svg/440px-Brian_Mulroney_Arms.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="600" data-file-height="659" /></a></span>
</td>
<td style="min-width:175px;">
<dl><dt>Crest</dt>
<dd>On a helmet mantled Azure doubled Or within a wreath of these colours issuant from a coronet érablé and flory Or a stone palace gate embattled comprising two Roman arches Azure and charged on the lintel with scales of justice Or.</dd>
<dt>Escutcheon</dt>
<dd>Azure on a pale Argent between four coronets érablé Or two and two a dexter hand appaumé and a maple leaf conjoined Gules the hand pointing towards chief the leaf towards base on a canton the Mark of the Prime Ministership of Canada (Argent four maple leaves conjoined in cross at the stem Gules).</dd>
<dt>Supporters</dt>
<dd>Two stallions Or crined and unguled Azure gorged with collars treflé on both edges Vert.</dd>
<dt>Compartment</dt>
<dd>On a grassy mound set with pine cones Or forming a bay rising above barry wavy Argent and Azure.</dd>
<dt>Motto</dt>
<dd>Multum In Amore Patriae Fides Multum Constantia<sup id="cite_ref-248" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-248">&#91;243&#93;</a></sup></dd></dl>
</td></tr></tbody></table>

<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Notes">Notes</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Brian_Mulroney&amp;action=edit&amp;section=45" title="Edit section: Notes"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1217336898"><div class="reflist reflist-lower-alpha">
<div class="mw-references-wrap"><ol class="references">
<li id="cite_note-45"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-45">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">Withdrawals made prior to May 22, 1985, must meet usage requirements to be received tax-free.</span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-48"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-48">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">They would otherwise be reincluded in the taxpayer's income if made between May 22, 1985, and December 31, 1985.</span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-136"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-136">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text"><a href="/wiki/Conservative_Party_of_Canada_(1867%E2%80%931942)" title="Conservative Party of Canada (1867–1942)">Conservative</a> Prime Minister <a href="/wiki/Robert_Borden" title="Robert Borden">Robert Borden</a> won two majority governments; the second majority he won in <a href="/wiki/1917_Canadian_federal_election" title="1917 Canadian federal election">1917</a> was when he was the leader of the <a href="/wiki/Unionist_Party_(Canada)" title="Unionist Party (Canada)">Unionist Party</a>, a party composed of pro-conscription Conservatives and Liberals.</span>
</li>
</ol></div></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1217336898"><div class="reflist">
<div class="mw-references-wrap"><ol class="references">
<li id="cite_note-111"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-111">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">In June 1987, all ten premiers agreed to ratify the Accord. Between then and June 1990, three anti-Meech premiers took office; <a href="/wiki/Frank_McKenna" title="Frank McKenna">Frank McKenna</a> became <a href="/wiki/Premier_of_New_Brunswick" title="Premier of New Brunswick">premier of New Brunswick</a> in October 1987, <a href="/wiki/Gary_Filmon" title="Gary Filmon">Gary Filmon</a> became <a href="/wiki/Premier_of_Manitoba" title="Premier of Manitoba">premier of Manitoba</a> in 1988, and <a href="/wiki/Clyde_Kirby_Wells" class="mw-redirect" title="Clyde Kirby Wells">Clyde Wells</a> became <a href="/wiki/Premier_of_Newfoundland_and_Labrador" title="Premier of Newfoundland and Labrador">premier of Newfoundland and Labrador</a> in 1989. The three premiers refused to support the Accord until their concerns were met in June 1990.</span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-116"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-116">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">The federal government would still have power over national groups such as the <a href="/wiki/Canadian_Broadcasting_Corporation" title="Canadian Broadcasting Corporation">Canadian Broadcasting Corporation</a> (CBC) and the <a href="/wiki/National_Film_Board" class="mw-redirect" title="National Film Board">National Film Board</a>.</span>
</li>
</ol></div></div>

<li><i>Spoils of Power: the Politics of Patronage</i>, by Jeffrey Simpson, 1988.</li>
<li><i>Friends in high places: politics and patronage in the Mulroney government</i>, by Claire Hoy, 1989.</li>
<li><i>Betrayal of Canada</i>, by Mel Hurtig, Stoddart Pub. Co., 1991, <link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1215172403"><a href="/wiki/ISBN_(identifier)" class="mw-redirect" title="ISBN (identifier)">ISBN</a>&#160;<a href="/wiki/Special:BookSources/0-7737-2542-3" title="Special:BookSources/0-7737-2542-3">0-7737-2542-3</a></li>

<li><i>Right Honourable Men: the Descent of Canadian Politics from Macdonald to Mulroney</i>, by <a href="/wiki/Michael_Bliss" title="Michael Bliss">Michael Bliss</a>, 1994.</li>
<li><i>On the Take: Crime, Corruption and Greed in the Mulroney Years</i>, by <a href="/wiki/Stevie_Cameron" title="Stevie Cameron">Stevie Cameron</a>, 1994.</li>
<li><i>The Prime Ministers of Canada</i>, by <a href="/wiki/Gordon_Donaldson_(journalist)" title="Gordon Donaldson (journalist)">Gordon Donaldson (journalist)</a>, 1997.</li>
<li><i>Promises, Promises: Breaking Faith in Canadian Politics</i>, by Anthony Hyde, 1997.</li>
<li><i>Presumed Guilty: Brian Mulroney, the Airbus Affair, and the Government of Canada</i>, by <a href="/wiki/William_Kaplan" title="William Kaplan">William Kaplan</a>, 1998.</li>
<li><i>The Last Amigo: Karlheinz Schreiber and the Anatomy of a Scandal</i>, by <a href="/wiki/Stevie_Cameron" title="Stevie Cameron">Stevie Cameron</a> and Harvey Cashore, 2001.</li>
<li><i>Egotists and Autocrats: The Prime Ministers of Canada</i>, by <a href="/wiki/George_Bowering" title="George Bowering">George Bowering</a>, 1999.</li>
<li><i>Bastards and Boneheads: Canada's Glorious Leaders, Past and Present</i>, by <a href="/wiki/Will_Ferguson" title="Will Ferguson">Will Ferguson</a>, 1999.</li>
<li><i>A Secret Trial: Brian Mulroney, Stevie Cameron, and the Public Trust</i>, by <a href="/wiki/William_Kaplan" title="William Kaplan">William Kaplan</a>, 2004.</li>
<li><i>The Secret Mulroney Tapes: Unguarded Confessions of a Prime Minister</i>, by <a href="/wiki/Peter_C._Newman" title="Peter C. Newman">Peter C. Newman</a>, 2005.</li></ul>
